is asymptotic normally distributed under model
\mathscr{M}_{\text{nonpar}}
with asymptotic variance
\mathrm{E}\Big\{\big[S^{\text{eff, nonpar}}(DM_{j})\big]^{2}\Big\}
.

•

If Assumption
7.4
also hold,
\widehat{IM}_{j}^{\text{avg},\,\text{QR}}
is the consistent estimator of
\overline{IM}_{j}
under the model
\mathscr{M}_{j,\,\text{union}}
for any
j\in[p]
. Furthermore, if Assumption
7.10
holds, then
\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{IM}_{j}^{\text{avg},\,\text{QR}}-\overline{IM}_{j}\big)
is asymptotically normally distributed under model
\mathscr{M}_{\text{nonpar}}
with asymptotic variance

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\Bigg\{\Bigg[\frac{1}{\#\operatorname{MEC}(\mathcal{C}_{M})}\sum_{\mathcal{G}_{M}\in\operatorname{MEC}(\mathcal{C}_{M})}S^{\text{eff, nonpar}}(IM_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M}))\Bigg]^{2}\Bigg\}.

An important result of Theorem
7.11
is that: for any
j\in[p]
, the quadruply robust estimators
\widehat{DM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}
and
\widehat{IM}_{j}^{\text{avg},\,\text{QR}}

are semiparametric locally efficient in the sense that they are regular and asymptotically linear under model
\mathscr{M}_{j,\,\text{union}}
, and achieve the
semiparametric efficiency bound for
{DM}_{j}
and
\overline{IM}_{j}
under model at the intersection submodel
\mathscr{M}_{0}\,\cap\,\mathscr{M}_{j,1}\,\cap\,\mathscr{M}_{j,2}\,\cap\,\mathscr{M}_{j,3}
. Hence, when all models are correct,
\widehat{DM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}
and
\widehat{IM}_{j}^{\text{avg},\,\text{QR}}
are semiparametric efficient in the model
\mathscr{M}_{\text{nonpar}}
at the
intersection submodel
\mathscr{M}_{0}\,\cap\,\mathscr{M}_{j,1}\,\cap\,\mathscr{M}_{j,2}\,\cap\,\mathscr{M}_{j,3}
by part iv in
Bickel and
Kwon 2001
for any
j\in[p]
.

8
Simulation Studies

In this section, we assess the finite-sample performance of our proposed quadruply robust estimators across two simulation scenarios.
The first scenario seeks to illustrate the robustness characteristics of our estimator in comparison to other estimation strategies, particularly when certain model specifications to a specific mediator are not met.
In the second simulation study, we demonstrate that our method can also be superior to any other estimation strategies in estimating the both direct and indirect interventional effects across all mediators, on average, within commonly adopted model configurations.

8.1
Simulation for a single mediator

We consider the finite-sample performance of the proposed quadruply robust estimators in comparison to the estimators under direct strategy, and the alternative strategies in Section
5
,
6.1.1
,
6.1.2
, and
6.1.3
for a single mediator.
We describe the detailed setting as follows: we set
t=p=3
, and fix the pre-specified
j
randomly sampled from
U\{[p]\}
. Then we design the following four data generating processes (DGPs), here
\Phi(\bm{\cdot})
and
\operatorname{logit}(\bm{\cdot})
are the standard normal distribution function and the inverse of the standard logistic function, and
B_{j:}
and
B_{-j:}
represent the
j
-th row of the matrix
B
and the matrix
B
with

j
-th row removed.

•

All correct:
C\,\leftarrow\,\mathcal{N}(0,I_{t-1})
,
A\,\leftarrow\,\mathds{1}\big\{U[0,1]\leq\Phi(\beta_{AC}^{\top}C)\big\}
,
M\,\leftarrow\,B_{MC}^{\top}C+\beta_{MA}A+B_{MM}^{\top}M+\mathcal{N}(0,I_{p})
, and
Y\,\leftarrow\,\beta_{YC}^{\top}C+\alpha_{YA}A+\beta_{YM}^{\top}M+\mathcal{N}(0,1)
;

•

\mathscr{M}_{0}
is correct: the exposure
A
comes from
A\,\leftarrow\,\mathds{1}(\operatorname{logit}(U[0,1])\leq\beta_{AC}^{\top}C)
instead;

•

\mathscr{M}_{j,\,1}
is correct: the outcome
Y
comes from
Y\,\leftarrow\,(\beta_{YC}^{\top}C+\alpha_{YA}A+\beta_{YM}^{\top}M)^{2/3}+\mathcal{N}(0,1)
instead;

•

\mathscr{M}_{j,\,2}
is correct: the mediators have the alternative structure
M_{j}\,\leftarrow\,\Theta_{MC,j:}C+\theta_{MA,j}A+\Big[(I-B_{MM}^{\top})^{-1}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{p})\Big]_{j}
and
M_{k}\,\leftarrow\,{(\Theta_{MA,k:}C+\theta_{MA,k}A)^{2/3}}+\Big[(I-B_{MM}^{\top})^{-1}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{p})\Big]_{k}
for
k\neq j
;

•

\mathscr{M}_{j,\,3}
is correct: the mediators have the alternative structure
M_{j}\,\leftarrow\,\penalty{\Theta_{MC,j:}C+\frac{1}{2}\theta_{MA,j}}+\Big[(I-B_{MM}^{\top})^{-1}\mathcal{N}(0,I_{p})\Big]_{j}
and
M_{-j}\,\leftarrow\,\Theta_{MC,-j:}C+\theta_{MA,-j}A+\Big[(I-B_{MM}^{\top})^{-1}\penalty\mathcal{N}(0,I_{p})\Big]_{-j}
.

Here the true adjacency matrix of mediators is generated from the Erdős-Rényi (ER) model with an expected degree as
\lfloor p/2\rfloor
, and the non-zero entries in
B_{MM}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
and all the elements in
\alpha_{YA},\beta_{MA},\beta_{YC},\beta_{YM},B_{MC}
are independently sampled from
U(-1,1)
. In each estimation method, we consistently treat
\mathscr{M}_{0}
as the underlying true model by default. We generate
n=1000
simulation samples, each comprising
N=100
independent observations, and the result for estimating the direct and indirect interventional effect of the pre-specified mediator
M_{j}
is shown in Table
1
. Here, we use the PC algorithm
(
Harris and
Drton 2013
)
to estimate the adjacency matrix of CPDAGs.

As illustrated in Table
1
, the simulation results align with the theoretical predictions made in previous sections. Specifically, when the entire distribution
F_{X}(\bm{\cdot})
is correctly specified, all estimators display consistency. However, in the presence of at least one misspecified component, only the quadruply robust estimator retains consistency. In contrast, one among the other estimators,
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}
for
\ell=0,1,2,3
, becomes inconsistent. Although we present only the continuous scenario in this part, our simulations under discrete
C
or
M
settings yielded similar outcomes. Importantly, under this simulation scenario, the estimator
\widehat{TM}_{j}^{\mathscr{M}_{0}}=\widehat{DM}_{j}^{\mathscr{M}_{0}}+\widehat{IM}_{j}^{\mathscr{M}_{0}}
corresponds precisely to the estimator utilized for the individual mediation effect
\eta_{j}
proposed in
Chakrabortty
et al. 2018
. Thus, our quadruply robust estimators outperform the estimator defined in
Chakrabortty
et al. 2018
.

8.2
Simulation for all mediators

Next, we consider the average performance of our quadruply robust estimators compared with other estimations under a fair model misspecification scenario in both continuous case (Section 1.4 in
Kang and
Schafer 2007
) and discrete case (Section 4.1 in
Xia and
Chan 2023
). The DGPs are defined as follows:

•

Continuous
M
:

Z\,\leftarrow\,\mathcal{N}(0,I_{t-1})
,
A\,\leftarrow\,\mathds{1}\{U[0,1]\leq\Phi(\beta_{AC}^{\top}Z)\}
,

M\,\leftarrow\,B_{MC}^{\top}Z+\beta_{MA}A+B_{MM}^{\top}M+\mathcal{N}\left(0,\left[\begin{matrix}\sigma_{1}^{2}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\sigma_{p}^{2}\end{matrix}\right]\right),

and
Y\,\leftarrow\,\beta_{YC}^{\top}Z+(\alpha_{YA}A+\beta_{YM}^{\top}M)^{2/3}+\mathcal{N}(0,1)
;

•

Discrete
M
:

C\,\leftarrow\,\mathcal{N}(0,I_{t-1})
,
A\,\leftarrow\,\mathds{1}\{U[0,1]\leq\Phi(\beta_{AC}^{\top}C)\}
,

M_{j}\,\leftarrow\,\mathds{1}\left\{\operatorname{logit}(U[0,1])\leq\Theta_{MC,j:}C+\theta_{MA,j}A+\left[(I-B_{MM}^{\top})^{-1}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\left[\begin{matrix}\sigma_{1}^{2}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&\sigma_{p}^{2}\end{matrix}\right]\right)\right]_{j}\right\},

and
Y\,\leftarrow\,\beta_{YC}^{\top}C+\alpha_{YA}A+\beta_{YM}^{\top}M+\beta_{YC}^{\top}AC+\beta_{YM}^{\top}AM+\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

Here
\sigma_{1}^{2},\ldots,\sigma_{p}^{2}
are independently drawn from the uniform distribution in
[0.5,1]
, whereas the other setting is the same as previous. In the continuous setting, instead of observing the
Z_{i}
’s, we observe
C_{i}
as the transformations of
Z_{i}
. We will always leave out the interaction and
x^{2/3}
when fitting
each model, and we also assume the link functions are all Probit.
For computations in the continuous
M
setting, we implement Algorithm
2
. While in the discrete
M
context, we employ Algorithm
1
, setting the Monte Carlo sample size to
L=100
.

As illustrated in Figure
1
, aside from the quadruply estimators (QR), other methods fail to yield consistent results. Furthermore, in most cases, our quadruply estimators exhibit a lower standard error compared to other methods. Thus, this also shows the robustness of our estimators.

Fig 1:
Simulation results for both continuous (top row) and discrete (bottom row) scenarios, showcasing the estimated average causal direct mediation effect (left column) and indirect mediation effect (right column) over
N=100
replications. The dots represent estimated averages, error bars detail the standard error derived from the replications, and the red line represents the true average value, calculated via numeric integrals according to Definition
3.2
.

9
Empirical Study

In this section, we illustrate our estimator in a real world application from AURORA study to explore the causal association of psychiatric disorders among trauma survivors, which is also studied in
Watson
et al. 2023
. In the study, our primary response of interest is the post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD), which was assessed three months post-trauma
Y
. The focal event, in this case, is the pre-trauma insomnia
A
that trauma survivors often experience:
A=1
represents survivor does have insomnia and
A=0
represents does not. The 4-dimensional potential mediator
M
including Peri-traumatic PT (PTSD), stress, acute distress (ASD), and depression, gauged two weeks subsequent to the traumatic incident, are included in our analysis. This study also accounts for various confounders
C
is a 9-dimensional vector such as age, gender, race, education level, pre-trauma physical and mental health, perceived stress level, neuroticism, and childhood trauma. The same as
Watson
et al. 2023
, before employing our methodology, categorical variables underwent one-hot encoding, numerical variables were centralized, and any missing data was excluded.
The total number of observations is
n=1494
with
t=10
and
p=4
.

(
Harris and

)
is shown in Figure

. Results from the quadruply robust estimators, as obtained using Algorithm

with Monte Carlo sample size
L=100
and a bootstrap number of
B=500
, along with other estimation methods employing a
\log n
truncation and the same Monte Carlo sample size and bootstrap number, are presented in Table

.

As demonstrated in Table
2
, for each mediator under consideration, a substantial discrepancy is observed between the estimates of
\mathscr{M}_{\ell_{1}}
and those of
\mathscr{M}_{\ell_{2}}
for
\ell_{2}\neq\ell_{1}
when employing any of the four estimation methods
\mathscr{M}_{\ell}
for
\ell=0,1,2,3
.
Moreover, none of these estimation methods manage to identify significant direct or indirect interventional effects for any of the mediators.
This highlights the pressing need for robust estimation approaches in this dataset. Notably, with the quadruply robust estimation, we discern that both the indirect interventional effect of acute distress and the direct interventional effect of peritraumatic PT are significant at the 95% confidence level, while other effects remain non-significant. These results also indicate that preventive intervention of 3-month PTSD after trauma exposure that focuses on reducing acute distress and peritraumatic PT is more likely to be effective for trauma survivors.

10
Discussion

The main contribution of this article is the introduction of direct and indirect interventional effects of mediators, alongside their semiparametric bounds and quadruply robust estimators. Our method accommodates continuous, categorical, and multivariate pre-treatments, mediators, and outcomes. Moreover, extending our methodology and theory to polytomous exposures is straightforward.
However, extending to continuous exposure, even under LSEMs, is non-trivial in theoretical sense. A potential method is suggested in
Cai
et al. 2021
to replace the indicator function
\mathds{1}(A=a)
with some kernel function
K((A-a)/h)
under bandwidth
h
, but as discussed in
Díaz and
van der Laan 2013
;
Kennedy
et al. 2017
;
Kennedy
et al. 2023
, pathwise differentiability will fail in this case, necessitating alternative estimation procedures and techniques.
On the other hand, note that our framework is dimensional-free, as long as the conditional densities and expectations meet the mild convergence rate, our quadruply robust estimations will always achieve semiparametric efficiency. However, in high-dimensional cases, non-parametric estimation mentioned in this article may not achieve the rate, necessitating additional assumptions like symmetry and shape constraints, as discussed in
Deng
et al. 2021
;
Xu and
Samworth 2021
;
Rodríguez-Casal and
Saavedra-Nieves 2022
. Introducing these assumptions still validates the semiparametric framework in our article under the full nonparametric model
\mathscr{M}_{\text{nonpar}}
, but our quadruply estimators may not be the most efficient under these added conditions.

B
Explicit formulas for Algorithm
1
and
2

We first give the formulas in Algorithm
1
, the formulas in Algorithm
2
will be shown in the proof of Proposition
6.1
later in this section. For calculating the formulas in Algorithm
1
, by Monte Carlo approximation, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{\zeta}_{j}^{\mathscr{M}_{0}}(a^{\prime},0,C)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}}\widehat{\mu}(C,1,m)\widehat{\pi}_{C,a^{\prime}}(m_{j})\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}(m_{-j})\,\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{M}}\widehat{\mu}(C,1,m)\frac{\widehat{\pi}_{C,a^{\prime}}(m_{j})\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}(m_{-j})}{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(m)}\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(m)\,\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}}^{(\ell)})\frac{\widehat{\pi}_{C,a^{\prime}}(M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0},j}^{(\ell)})\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}(M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0},-j}^{(\ell)})}{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}}^{(\ell)})}.

where the sampling density
\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(m)
is calculated by
\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(m)=\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(m_{j})\widehat{\pi}_{C,1,m_{j}}(m_{-j})
and does not need to estimate additionally.
Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\big\langle\widehat{\tau}_{C,\bm{\cdot}\,;\,j}(C,1,M_{-j})\big\rangle\approx\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\left[\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}}^{(\ell)},M_{-j})-\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}}^{(\ell)},M_{-j})\right]

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\tau}_{C,0\,;\,-j}(C,1,M_{j})\approx\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M_{j},M_{-j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}}^{(\ell)}).

Note that we can rewrite
\widehat{DM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{QR}_{DM_{j}}(X_{i})
with

\displaystyle\widehat{QR}_{DM_{j}}(X)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\frac{\big\langle\widehat{\pi}_{C,\bm{\cdot}}(M_{-j})\big\rangle}{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1,M_{j}}(M_{-j})}\big[Y-\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M)\big]+\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\big\langle\widehat{\tau}_{C,\bm{\cdot}\,;\,j}(C,1,M_{-j})\big\rangle

\displaystyle+\bigg\langle\frac{\mathds{1}(A=\bm{\cdot})}{\widehat{e}_{\bm{\cdot}}(C)}\bigg\rangle\widehat{\tau}_{C,0\,;\,-j}(C,1,M_{j})+\bigg[1-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\bigg]\widehat{\zeta}_{j}^{\mathscr{M}_{0}}(1,0,C)

\displaystyle+\left[2\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}-1\right]\widehat{\zeta}_{j}^{\mathscr{M}_{0}}(0,0,C).

Therefore, we have the approximation as
\widehat{DM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}\approx\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{QR}_{DM_{j}}^{\text{MC}}(X_{i})
with the following explicit formula

\displaystyle\widehat{QR}_{DM_{j}}^{\text{MC}}(X)

\displaystyle:=\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\bigg[\frac{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(M_{-j})-\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}(M_{-j})}{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1,M_{j}}(M_{-j})}\bigg]\big\{Y-\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M)\big\}

(B.1)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\left[\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}}^{(\ell)},M_{-j})-\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}}^{(\ell)},M_{-j})\right]

\displaystyle+\bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\bigg]\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M_{j},M_{-j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}}^{(\ell)})

\displaystyle+\bigg[1-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\bigg]\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}}^{(\ell)})\frac{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0},j}^{(\ell)})\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}(M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0},-j}^{(\ell)})}{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}}^{(\ell)})}

\displaystyle+\left[2\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}-1\right]\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\widehat{\mu}(C,1,M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}}^{(\ell)})\frac{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}(M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0},j}^{(\ell)})\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}(M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,0},-j}^{(\ell)})}{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}(M_{\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}}^{(\ell)})}.

Similarly, note that the double integral can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C\,;\,\mathcal{G}_{M})\mid C\big]

\displaystyle=\int\mu\big(a^{\prime},m_{j},\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M}),C\big)\pi_{C,a^{\prime}}\big(\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big)\pi_{C}(m_{j})\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M}))

\displaystyle\approx\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell_{2}=1}^{N}\mu\big(a^{\prime},M_{j,\pi_{C}}^{(\ell_{1})},\operatorname{Pa}_{j,\pi_{C,a^{\prime},\mathcal{G}_{M}}}^{(\ell_{2})},C\big).

Thus, given the fact
\widehat{TM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}(\mathcal{G}_{M})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{QR}_{TM_{j}}(X_{i}\,;\,\mathcal{G}_{M})
with

\displaystyle\widehat{QR}_{TM_{j}}(X\,;\,\mathcal{G}_{M})

\displaystyle:=\left\langle\frac{\mathds{1}(A=\bm{\cdot})}{\widehat{e}_{\bm{\cdot}}(C)}\big[Y-\widehat{\mu}(C,\bm{\cdot})\big]-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=\bm{\cdot})}{\widehat{e}_{\bm{\cdot}}(C)}\widehat{\pi}_{C,\bm{\cdot}}\big(\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big)\Big(Y-\widehat{\mu}(C,\bm{\cdot},\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M}),M_{j})\Big)\right\rangle

\displaystyle-\left\langle\frac{\mathds{1}(A=\bm{\cdot})}{\widehat{e}_{\bm{\cdot}}(C)}\Big\{\widehat{\tau}_{C\,;\,j}\big(C,1,\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big)-{\mathrm{E}}\big[\widehat{\varrho}_{j}(\bm{\cdot},M_{j},C\,;\,\mathcal{G}_{M})\mid C\big]\Big\}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\widehat{\mu}(C,\bm{\cdot})-\widehat{\tau}_{C,\bm{\cdot}\,;\,\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}(C,\bm{\cdot},M_{j})\right\rangle,

we have the approximation
\widehat{DM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}\approx\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{QR}_{TM_{j}}^{\text{MC}}(X_{i})
, where

\displaystyle\widehat{QR}_{TM_{j}}^{\text{MC}}(X\,;\,\mathcal{G}_{M}):=

\displaystyle\Bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\Big\{Y-\widehat{\mu}(C,1)\Big\}-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\Big\{Y-\widehat{\mu}(C,0)\big]\Big\}\Bigg]

(B.2)

\displaystyle-\Bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\widehat{\pi}_{C,1}\big(\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big)\Big\{Y-\widehat{\mu}(C,1,\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M}),M_{j})\Big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\widehat{\pi}_{C,0}\big(\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big)\Big\{Y-\widehat{\mu}(C,0,\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M}),M_{j})\Big\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell_{2}=1}^{N}\Big[\widehat{\mu}\big(C,1,M_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C}}^{(\ell_{1})},\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big)-\widehat{\mu}\big(C,1,M_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C}}^{(\ell_{1})},\operatorname{Pa}_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,1,\mathcal{G}_{M}}}^{(\ell_{2})}\big)\Big]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N^{2}}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{N}\sum_{\ell_{2}=1}^{N}\Big[\widehat{\mu}\big(C,0,M_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C}}^{(\ell_{1})},\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big)-\widehat{\mu}\big(C,0,M_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C}}^{(\ell_{1})},\operatorname{Pa}_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,0,\mathcal{G}_{M}}}^{(\ell_{2})},C\big)\Big]\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\widehat{\mu}(C,1)-\widehat{\mu}(C,0)-\bigg[\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\Big[\widehat{\mu}\big(C,1,\operatorname{Pa}_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,1,\mathcal{G}_{M}}}^{(\ell)},M_{j}\big)-\widehat{\mu}\big(C,0,\operatorname{Pa}_{j,\widehat{\pi}_{C,0,\mathcal{G}_{M}}}^{(\ell)},M_{j}\big)\bigg].

Proof of Proposition
6.1
:

Denote
\phi(\bm{\cdot}\,;\mu,\Sigma)
as the density of distribution
\mathcal{N}(\mu,\Sigma)
. Similar to (
6.10
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\mu(C,1,m_{j},M_{-j})\pi_{C,1}(m_{j})\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\Big[\beta_{YC}C+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM,j}m_{j}+\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}M_{-j}\Big]\phi\Big(m_{j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\theta_{MA,j},\,\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{jj}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{YC}C+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM,j}\Big\{\big[\widehat{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}\Big\}+\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}M_{-j},

thus

\displaystyle\big\langle\tau_{C,\bm{\cdot}\,;\,j}(C,1,M_{-j})\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\Big[\beta_{YC}C+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM,j}\Big\{\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\theta_{MA,j}\Big\}+\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}M_{-j}\Big]

\displaystyle-\Big[\beta_{YC}C+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM,j}\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}M_{-j}\Big]=\beta_{YM,j}\theta_{MA,j}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{-j}}{\mu}(C,1,M_{j},m_{-j})\pi_{C,0}(m_{-j})\,\mathrm{d}m_{-j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\Big[\beta_{YC}C+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM,j}M_{j}+\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}m_{-j}\Big]\phi\Big(m_{-j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{-j},\,\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{-j,-j}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}m_{-j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{YC}C+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM,j}M_{j}+\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{-j}

and

\displaystyle\zeta_{j}(a^{\prime},0,C)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}}\mu(C,1,m)\pi_{C,a^{\prime}}(m_{j})\pi_{C,0}(m_{-j})\,\mathrm{d}m

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}}\Big[\beta_{YC}C+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM,j}m_{j}+\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}m_{-j}\Big]\phi\Big(m_{j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\theta_{MA,j}a^{\prime},\,\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{j,j}\Big)

\displaystyle\phi\Big(m_{-j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{-j},\,\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{-j,-j}\Big)\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}\,\mathrm{d}m_{-j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta_{YC}C+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM,j}\Big\{\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\theta_{MA,j}a^{\prime}\Big\}+\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{-j}.

Therefore, under Assumption
5.1
and Gaussian assumption,

\widehat{DM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}=\mathbb{P}_{n}\widehat{QR}_{DM_{j}}^{\text{fast}}(X)+\widehat{DM}_{j}^{\text{OLS}},

where the (estimated) quadruply adjustment terms
\widehat{QR}_{DM_{j}}(X)
is defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{QR}_{DM_{j}}^{\text{fast}}(X)=\widehat{QR}_{DM_{j}}^{\text{fast}}(C,A,M,Y)

(B.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\bigg[\frac{\phi\big(M_{-j};\big[\widehat{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{-j}+\widehat{\theta}_{MA,-j},\big[\widehat{\operatorname{var}}(e_{M})\big]_{-j,-j}\big)-\phi\big(M_{-j};\big[\widehat{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{-j},\big[\widehat{\operatorname{var}}(e_{M})\big]_{-j,-j}\big)}{\phi\big(M_{-j}\mid M_{j};\big[\widehat{\Theta}_{MC}C+\widehat{\theta}_{MA}\big]_{-j\,\mid\,j},\big[\widehat{\operatorname{var}}(e_{M})\big]_{-j\,\mid\,j}\big)}\bigg]

\displaystyle\times\Big\{Y-\big[\widehat{\beta}_{YC}C+\widehat{\alpha}_{YA}+\widehat{\beta}_{YM}^{\top}M\big]\Big\}

\displaystyle+\bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\bigg]\Big[\widehat{\beta}_{YC}C+\widehat{\alpha}_{YA}+\widehat{\beta}_{YM,j}M_{j}+\widehat{\beta}_{YM,-j}^{\top}\big[{\widehat{\Theta}}_{MC}C\big]_{-j}\Big]

\displaystyle-\bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\Big[\widehat{\beta}_{YC}^{\top}C+\widehat{\alpha}_{YA}+\widehat{\beta}_{YM,j}\Big\{\big[{\widehat{\Theta}}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\widehat{\theta}_{MA,j}\Big\}+\widehat{\beta}_{YM,-j}^{\top}\big[{\widehat{\Theta}}_{MC}C\big]_{-j}\Big]

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\Big[\widehat{\beta}_{YC}^{\top}C+\widehat{\alpha}_{YA}+\widehat{\beta}_{YM,j}\big[{\widehat{\Theta}}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\widehat{\beta}_{YM,-j}^{\top}\big[{\widehat{\Theta}}_{MC}C\big]_{-j}\Big]\bigg].

Similarly, we can show that

\displaystyle\pi_{C}(M_{j})

\displaystyle=e_{0}(C)\pi_{C,0}(M_{j})+e_{1}(C)\pi_{C,1}(M_{j})

\displaystyle=e_{0}(C)\phi\Big(m_{j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j},\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{j,j}\Big)+e_{1}(C)\phi\Big(m_{j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\theta_{MA,j},\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{j,j}\Big),

\displaystyle\pi_{C,a^{\prime}}\big(\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big)=\phi\Big(\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{k:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}+\theta_{MA,k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}a^{\prime},\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{kk:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}\Big),

\displaystyle\tau_{C\,;\,j}\big(C,a^{\prime},\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big)=\int_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\Big\{\eta_{YM_{j}}m_{j}+\gamma_{Y\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}^{\top}\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})+\eta_{YA}a^{\prime}+\gamma_{YC}^{\top}C\Big\}

\displaystyle\Big\{e_{0}(C)\phi\Big(m_{j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j},\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{j,j}\Big)+e_{1}(C)\phi\Big(m_{j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\theta_{MA,j},\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{j,j}\Big)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma_{Y\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}^{\top}\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})+\eta_{YA}a^{\prime}+\gamma_{YC}^{\top}C+\eta_{YM_{j}}\Big[e_{0}(C)\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+e_{1}(C)\big(\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\theta_{MA,j}\big)\Big],

and

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{E}}\big[\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C\,;\,\mathcal{G}_{M})\mid C\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{pa}_{j}}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\int_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}{\mu}\big(C,a^{\prime},\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M}),m_{j}\big){\pi}_{C,a^{\prime}}\big(\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\big){\pi}_{C}(m_{j})\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{M}_{\operatorname{pa}_{j}}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\int_{\mathcal{M}_{j}}\Big\{\eta_{YM_{j}}m_{j}+\gamma_{Y\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}^{\top}\operatorname{pa}_{j}+\eta_{YA}a^{\prime}+\gamma_{YC}^{\top}C\Big\}

\displaystyle\times\phi\Big(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{k:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}+\theta_{MA,k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}a^{\prime},\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{kk:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big\{e_{0}(C)\phi\Big(m_{j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j},\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{j,j}\Big)+e_{1}(C)\phi\Big(m_{j}\,;\,\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+\theta_{MA,j},\big[\operatorname{var}(e_{M})\big]_{j,j}\Big)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{YA}a^{\prime}+\gamma_{YC}^{\top}C+\gamma_{Y\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}^{\top}\Big\{\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{k:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}+\theta_{MA,k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}a^{\prime}\Big\}

\displaystyle+\eta_{YM_{j}}\Big\{e_{0}(C)\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+e_{1}(C)\big[{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{j}+e_{1}(C)\theta_{MA,j}\Big\}.

Note that
\mu(C,a^{\prime})=\gamma_{YA}^{{\dagger}}a^{\prime}+\eta_{YC}^{{\dagger}\top}C
.
Hence, we conclude

\widehat{TM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}(\mathcal{G}_{M})=\mathbb{P}_{n}\widehat{QR}_{TM_{j}}^{\text{fast}}(X;\mathcal{G}_{M})+\widehat{TM}_{j}^{\text{OLS}},

with the estimated adjustment term
\widehat{QR}_{TM_{j}}^{\text{fast}}(X;\mathcal{G}_{M})
defined as

\displaystyle\widehat{QR}_{TM_{j}}^{\text{fast}}(X;\mathcal{G}_{M})=\Bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\Big\{Y-\widehat{\gamma}_{YA}^{{\dagger}}-\widehat{\eta}_{YC}^{{\dagger}\top}C\Big\}-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\Big\{Y-\widehat{\eta}_{YC}^{{\dagger}\top}C\big]\Big\}\Bigg]

(B.4)

\displaystyle-\Bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\phi\Big(\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\,;\,\big[\widehat{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{k:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}+\widehat{\theta}_{MA,k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})},\big[\widehat{\operatorname{var}}(e_{M})\big]_{kk:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big\{Y-\widehat{\eta}_{YM_{j}}M_{j}-\widehat{\gamma}_{Y\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}^{\top}\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})-\widehat{\eta}_{YA}A-\widehat{\gamma}_{YC}^{\top}C\Big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\phi\Big(\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\,;\,\big[\widehat{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{k:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})},\big[\widehat{\operatorname{var}}(e_{M})\big]_{kk:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}\Big)

\displaystyle\times\Big\{Y-\widehat{\eta}_{YM_{j}}M_{j}-\widehat{\gamma}_{Y\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}^{\top}\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})-\widehat{\gamma}_{YC}^{\top}C\Big\}

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{\widehat{e}_{1}(C)}\Big\{\widehat{\gamma}_{Y\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}^{\top}\Big[\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})-\big[\widehat{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{k:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}-\widehat{\theta}_{MA,k:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}\Big]\Big\}

\displaystyle-\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{\widehat{e}_{0}(C)}\Big\{\widehat{\gamma}_{Y\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}^{\top}\Big[\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})-\big[\widehat{\Theta}_{MC}C\big]_{k:k\in\operatorname{pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})}\Big]\Big\}\Bigg]

where
\phi(\bm{\cdot}\mid X_{S_{1}}\,;\,\mu_{S_{2}\mid S_{1}},\Sigma_{S_{2}\mid S_{1}})
is the density for the conditional distribution
X_{S_{2}}\mid X_{S_{1}}
where
(X_{S_{1}}^{\top},X_{S_{2}}^{\top})^{\top}\,\sim\,\mathcal{N}(\mu,\Sigma)
.
Finally, by
\widehat{IM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}(\mathcal{G}_{M})=\widehat{TM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}(\mathcal{G}_{M})-\widehat{DM}_{j}^{\text{QR}}
, we conclude the proposition when both linear structures hold in Assumption
5.1
. Finally, it is noteworthy, as seen in Theorem
7.11
, that even if only one linear assumption holds in Assumption
5.1
, our estimator remains consistent.
∎

C
Technique Proofs

In the proof, to avoid any misunderstanding, we abbreviate the conditional density
f_{Z_{2}\mid Z_{1}}(z_{2}\mid z_{1})
as
f(z_{2}\mid z_{1})
, and omit the interval of the integral, assuming the interval is the support of the integrand variable.

C.1
The Proofs of Section
4

Proof of Theorem
4.1
:

Fixing a DAG
\mathcal{G}_{M}
, we drop the DAG index in these quantities for simplicity. Note that the joint intervention density can be written as

\displaystyle f\big(y\mid do(A=a,\,M_{j}=m_{j})\big)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{f(y,c,a,m_{j},\operatorname{pa}_{j})}{f(a\mid c)f(m_{j}\mid c,a,\operatorname{pa}_{j})}\,\mathrm{d}c\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}

\displaystyle=\int\frac{f(y,c,a,m_{j},\operatorname{pa}_{j})}{f(m_{j},c,a,\operatorname{pa}_{j})}\frac{f(c,a,\operatorname{pa}_{j})}{f(a,c)}f(c)\,\mathrm{d}c\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}

\displaystyle=\int f(y\mid c,a,m_{j},\operatorname{pa}_{j})f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid c,a)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}c\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j},

where the first equation is applying
\operatorname{Pa}(A)=C
, and
\operatorname{Pa}(M_{j})=(C,A,\operatorname{Pa}_{j})
with Theorem 6 in
Kuroki and
Miyakawa 1999
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid do(A=a,\,M_{j}=m_{j}),C\big]

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a,M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a,C)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}

\displaystyle=\varrho_{j}(a,m_{j},C),

then

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid do(A=1,M_{j}),C=c\big]-\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid do(A=0,M_{j}),C=c\big]=\varrho_{j}(1,M_{j},c)-\varrho_{j}(0,M_{j},c).

Denote

\displaystyle TM_{j}(c):=

\displaystyle\Big\{\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,C=c\big]-\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=0,C=c\big]\Big\}

\displaystyle-\Big\{\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid do(A=1,M_{j}),C=c\big]-\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid do(A=0,M_{j}),C=c\big]\Big\}.

Since
TM_{j}=\mathrm{E}TM_{j}(C)
, we have

\displaystyle TM_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\big[TM_{j}(C)\big]

\displaystyle=\Big\{\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,C\big]-\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=0,C\big]\Big\}-\Big\{\varrho_{j}(1,M_{j},C)-\varrho_{j}(0,M_{j},C)\Big\}.

The formula for
DM_{j}
is just by the definition.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.3
:

Let
F_{X;t}
denote a one dimensional regular parametric submodel of
\mathscr{M}_{\text{nonpar}}
, with
F_{X;0}=F_{X}
, and let
\mathrm{E}_{t}
be the expectation with respect to
F_{X;t}
.
Denote
U
the score of
F_{X;t}
at
t=0
and
\nabla_{t=0}
denoting differentiation with respect to
t
at
t=0
. For a fixed DAG
\mathcal{G}_{M}
, we drop
\mathcal{G}_{M}
in
\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C;\mathcal{G}_{M})
as
\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C)
. Note that we can write

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int f(m_{j},c)\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)\int\mu(C,a^{\prime},\operatorname{pa}_{j},M_{j})\,\pi_{C,a^{\prime}}(\operatorname{pa}_{j})\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j}\mid c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}\,\mathrm{d}c.

Denote
\mathrm{d}(c,\operatorname{pa}_{j},m_{j})=\mathrm{d}\mu
, then we can get

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathrm{E}_{t}\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\nabla_{t=0}\mathrm{E}_{t}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j}\mid c)f(c)\mathrm{d}\mu\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]\nabla_{t=0}f_{t}(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j}\mid c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)\nabla_{t=0}f_{t}(m_{j}\mid c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j}\mid c)\nabla_{t=0}f_{t}(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{4}A_{j,k}.

Here
A_{j,k},k=1,2,3,4
can be calculated straightforwardly by the method in
Tchetgen and
Shpitser 2012
. First,

\displaystyle A_{j,1}=\int\nabla_{t}\mathrm{E}_{t}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j}\mid c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\int\frac{\mathds{1}(a^{\prime}=A,m_{j}=M_{j},\operatorname{pa}_{j}=\operatorname{Pa}_{j},c=C)}{f(c)f(a^{\prime}\mid c)f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j}\mid\operatorname{pa}_{j},a^{\prime},c)}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{Y-\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]\Big\}f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j},c)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\int\frac{\mathds{1}(a^{\prime}=A,m_{j}=M_{j},\operatorname{pa}_{j}=\operatorname{Pa}_{j},c=C)f(m_{j}\mid c)}{f(a^{\prime}\mid c)f(m_{j}\mid\operatorname{pa}_{j},a^{\prime},c)}

\displaystyle\times\Big\{Y-\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{e_{a^{\prime}}(C)}\pi_{C,a^{\prime}}(\operatorname{Pa}_{j})\Big\{Y-\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j},C\big]\Big\}\Bigg].

Similarly, one can easily obtain that

\displaystyle A_{j,2}=\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]\nabla_{t}f_{t}(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j},c)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]

\displaystyle\times\frac{\mathds{1}(a^{\prime}=A,c=C)}{f(a^{\prime}\mid c)f(c)}\Big\{\mathds{1}(\operatorname{pa}_{j}=\operatorname{Pa}_{j})-f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)\Big\}f(m_{j}\mid c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}\,\mathrm{d}c\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{e_{a^{\prime}}(C)}\bigg\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid C,A=a^{\prime},\operatorname{Pa}_{j},M_{j}=m_{j}\big]f(m_{j}\mid C)\,dm_{j}-\mathrm{E}\big[\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C)\mid C\big]\bigg\}\Bigg],

\displaystyle A_{j,3}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)\nabla_{t}f_{t}(m_{j}\mid c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)

\displaystyle\times\frac{\mathds{1}(c=C)}{f(c)}\Big\{\mathds{1}(m_{j}=M_{j})-f(m_{j}\mid c)\Big\}\,f(c)\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mathrm{d}c\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\Big[U\big\{\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C)-\mathrm{E}\big[\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C)\mid C\big]\big\}\Big],

and

\displaystyle A_{j,4}

\displaystyle=\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j}\mid c)\nabla_{t}f_{t}(c)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=a^{\prime},M_{j}=m_{j},{\operatorname{Pa}}_{j}=\operatorname{pa}_{j},C=c\big]

\displaystyle\times f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j}\mid c)\big\{\mathds{1}(c=C)-f(c)\big\}\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\Big[U\big\{\mathrm{E}\big[\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C)\mid C\big]-\mathrm{E}\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C)\big\}\Big].

Similarly, we can decompose

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathrm{E}_{t}\zeta_{j}(a^{\prime},0,C)\right|_{t=0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\nabla_{t=0}\mathrm{E}_{t}\big[Y\mid A=1,M=m,C=c\big]f(m_{-j}\mid A=0,C=c)f(m_{j}\mid A=1,C=c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{E}_{t}\big[Y\mid A=1,M=m,C=c\big]\nabla_{t=0}f(m_{-j}\mid A=0,C=c)f(m_{j}\mid A=1,C=c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{E}_{t}\big[Y\mid A=1,M=m,C=c\big]f(m_{-j}\mid A=0,C=c)\nabla_{t=0}f(m_{j}\mid A=1,C=c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle+\int\mathrm{E}_{t}\big[Y\mid A=1,M=m,C=c\big]f(m_{-j}\mid A=0,C=c)f(m_{j}\mid A=1,C=c)\nabla_{t=0}f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu\,\mathrm{d}F_{X,t}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{4}B_{j,k},

and similarly verify that

B_{j,1}=\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{f(A=1\mid C)}\frac{f(M_{j}\mid A=0,C)f(M_{-j}\mid A=a^{\prime},C)}{f(M_{j}\mid A=1,C,M_{-j})f(M_{-j}\mid A=1,C)}\Big\{Y-\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,M,C\big]\Big\}\Bigg],

B_{j,2}=\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{f(A=0\mid C)}\bigg\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,M_{-j},C,M_{j}=m_{j}\big]f(m_{j}\mid A=a^{\prime},C=c)\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}-\zeta_{j}(a^{\prime},0,C)\bigg\}\Bigg],

B_{j,3}=\mathrm{E}\Bigg[U\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{f(A=a^{\prime}\mid C)}\bigg\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,M_{j},C,M_{-j}=m_{-j}\big]f(m_{-j}\mid A=0,C)\,\mathrm{d}m_{-j}-\zeta_{j}(a^{\prime},0,C)\bigg\}\Bigg],

and

B_{j,4}=\mathrm{E}\Big[U\big(\zeta_{j}(a^{\prime},0,C)-\mathrm{E}\zeta_{j}(a^{\prime},0,C)\big)\Big].

Finally, the efficient score for
\mathrm{E}\kappa(a^{\prime},C)
equal to

\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{e_{a^{\prime}}(C)}\Big\{Y-\kappa(a^{\prime},C)\Big\}+\kappa(a^{\prime},C)-\mathrm{E}\kappa(a^{\prime},C)

has been well studied in various literature, see Section 2.2 in
Tchetgen and
Shpitser 2012
for example. Thus, we conclude the results in the theorem.
∎

Proof of Corollary
4.4
:

The results are directly derived due to the linear property of efficient scores.
∎

C.2
The Proofs of Section
5

Let
\mathrm{E}_{\xi}[\bm{\cdot}]
represent the expected value computed with respect to the random variable
\xi
, while treating other variables as constants.
Employing graphical techniques, we can demonstrate the lemma as follows.

Lemma C.1
.

Suppose the model satisfies Assumption
5.1
, then natural effects defined in Definition
3.1
satisfy

\displaystyle TE=\mathrm{E}_{C}\big[\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=1),C\}-\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=0),C\}\big],

DE=\mathrm{E}_{C}\big[\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=1,M=m^{(0)}),C\}-\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=0),C\}\big],

and

IE=\mathrm{E}_{C}\big[\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=0,M=m^{(1)}),C\}-\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=0),C\}\big].

Proof of Lemma
C.1
:

Introduce
A^{{\dagger}}
has the same law as
A
except that
A^{{\dagger}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 3.46875pt{}\kern 3.46875pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\textstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 3.46875pt{}\kern 3.46875pt\hbox{$\textstyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 2.48856pt{}\kern 2.48856pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 1.78203pt{}\kern 1.78203pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$}}}C
, then by the definition of
do
operator,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{C}\big[\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=a),C\}\big]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid A^{{\dagger}}=a,C\}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid A^{{\dagger}}=a\}=\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=a)\}.

for any
a\in\{0,1\}
This furthermore implies

\displaystyle TE

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=1)\}-\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=0)\}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}\big[\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=1),C\}\big]-\mathrm{E}_{C}\big[\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=0),C\}\big].

This proves the result of
TE
. We can similarly prove the result for
DE
and
IE
.
∎

Lemma
C.1
shows the definition of natural effects defined in
Pearl 2000
can actually be written as the average on confounders.
We require some DAG lemmas in order to study the formula for indirect effects of mediators. To the best of our knowledge, these lemmas are also novel, and they can be helpful resources for pertinent research.

Lemma C.2
.

Suppose
B
is the weight matrix of a DAG on
\{X_{1},\ldots,X_{q}\}
. Define
\Delta_{-i}:=\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}u\big]_{-i}-(I_{q-1}-B^{\top}_{-i,-i})^{-1}u_{-i}
, then

(1)

(Path Representation)

\Delta_{-i}=u_{i}\Bigg[\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\mathrm{E}[X_{j}\mid do_{B}(X_{i}=x_{i})]\Bigg]_{j\neq i}+\Bigg[\sum_{k\neq i}u_{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{k}}\mathrm{E}[X_{j}\mid do_{B_{-i,-i}}(X_{k}=x_{k})]\Bigg]_{j\neq i},

where
do_{A}
is the
do
operator on the DAG weight matrix
A
.

(2)

(Matrix Expression)

\Delta_{-i}=u_{i}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}+\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{i,-i}u_{-i}.

Before proving this lemma, we need the following result for digraph (do not require acyclic). We denote
|w(x)|
as the product of the weights of the edges for any path
x
.

Lemma C.3
(
Yu
et al. 2019
).

If
A
is an adjacency matrix of a weighted digraph on
m
vertices, then

\left[A^{k}\right]_{ij}=\sum_{x\in\pi_{ij}^{+}(k)}|w(x)|-\sum_{y\in\pi_{ij}^{-}(k)}|w(y)|,

where
\pi_{ij}^{+}(k)
is the set of path with positive direction from
i
to
j
and
\pi_{ij}^{-}(k)
is the set of path with negative direction from
i
to
j
with length
k
.

Proof of Lemma
C.2
:

Denote
j
as the major index. And for any nonempty set
S=\{l_{1},\ldots,l_{|S|}\}
, we denote

\big[u_{j}\big]_{S}=\big[u_{j}\big]_{j\in S}=(u_{l_{1}},\ldots,u_{l_{|S|}})^{\top}

as a vector with dimension
|S|
. Specially, define
[u_{j}]:=[u_{j}]_{j\in[q]}
. By lemma
C.3
, for any
u\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
, we have

\displaystyle(I-B^{\top})^{-1}u

\displaystyle=u+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\big[B^{\top}\big]^{m}u

\displaystyle=[u_{j}]+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{q}\big[(B^{m})^{\top}\big]_{jk}u_{k}\bigg]

\displaystyle=[u_{j}]+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{q}[B^{m}]_{kj}u_{k}\bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{By applying Lemma \ref{lem_digra}}}{=}[u_{j}]+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{q}u_{k}\sum_{\Pi\in\pi_{kj}^{(m)}}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\bigg]

\displaystyle=[u_{j}]+\sum_{k=1}^{q}u_{k}\bigg[\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\sum_{\Pi\in\pi_{kj}^{(m)}}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\bigg]

\displaystyle=\Bigg[u_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{q}u_{k}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{kj}}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg],

where we drop the superscript in the path
\pi_{kj}^{+}
by the fact that there is no negative path in DAG. Similarly, one can prove

(I-B_{-i,-i}^{\top})^{-1}u_{-i}=\Bigg[u_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{p}u_{k}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{kj}\text{ and }i\notin\Pi}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\Delta_{-i}

\displaystyle=\big[(I-B^{\top})^{-1}u\big]_{-i}-(I-B^{\top}_{-i,-i})^{-1}u_{-i}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[u_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{q}u_{k}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{kj}}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}-\Bigg[u_{j}+\sum_{k=1}^{p}u_{k}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{kj}\text{ and }i\notin\Pi}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\sum_{k=1}^{p}u_{k}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{kj}\text{ and }i\in\Pi}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[u_{i}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{ij}}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|+\sum_{k\neq i}^{q}u_{k}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{kj}\text{ and }i\notin\Pi}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[u_{i}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{ij}}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}+\Bigg[\sum_{k\neq i}^{q}u_{k}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{kj}\text{ and }i\notin\Pi}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=:\Delta_{-i,1}+\Delta_{-i,2}.

By the definition of
do
operator and Proposition 3.1 in the Supplementary of
Nandy
et al. 2017
, one have

\displaystyle\Delta_{-i,1}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[u_{i}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{ij}}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=u_{i}\Bigg[\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{ij}}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=u_{i}\Bigg[\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\mathrm{E}[X_{j}\mid do_{B}(X_{i}=x_{i})]\Bigg]_{j\neq i}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\Delta_{-i,2}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\sum_{k\neq i}^{q}u_{k}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{kj}\text{ and }i\notin\Pi}\big|w_{B}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\sum_{k\neq i}^{q}u_{k}\sum_{\exists\,\Pi\in\pi_{kj}(B_{-i,-i})}\big|w_{B_{-i,-i}}(\Pi)\big|\Bigg]_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\sum_{k\neq i}u_{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{k}}\mathrm{E}[X_{j}\mid do_{B_{-i,-i}}(X_{k}=x_{k})]\Bigg]_{j\neq i},

This proves the path representation. For the matrix representation, we use Woodbury matrix identity

\left(A_{-j,-j}^{-1}\right)^{-1}=\left[A^{-1}\right]_{-j,-j}-\left[A^{-1}\right]_{-j,j}\left(\left[A^{-1}\right]_{jj}\right)^{-1}\left[A^{-1}\right]_{j-j}.

Thus, take
A=(I_{q}-B^{\top})
, we have

\displaystyle\left(A_{-i,-i}^{-1}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\left(I_{q-1}-B_{-i,-i}\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,-i}-\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}\Big(\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{i,i}\Big)^{-1}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{i,-i}

\displaystyle=\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,-i}-\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{i,-i}.

where we use the fact that
[A^{-1}]_{i,i}\equiv 1
for
i\in[p]
as
B^{\top}
is acyclic. Then,

\displaystyle\left(I_{q-1}-B_{-i,-i}\right)^{-1}u_{-i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,-i}u_{-i}-\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{i,-i}u_{-i}.

On the other hand, note that, for any
j\neq i
,
(Au)_{j}=\sum_{k=1}^{q}A_{jk}u_{k}
. Thus,

\displaystyle{}_{-i}

\displaystyle=\big[Au\big]_{j\neq i}=\left[A_{ji}u_{i}+\sum_{k\neq i}A_{jk}u_{k}\right]_{j\neq i}

\displaystyle=u_{i}[A]_{-i,i}+A_{-i,-i}u_{-i}.

Note that

\displaystyle\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}u\big]_{-i}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{i}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}+\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,-i}u_{-i}.

Combine the two results, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Delta_{-i}

\displaystyle=\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}u\big]_{-i}-(I_{q-1}-B^{\top}_{-i,-i})^{-1}u_{-i}

\displaystyle=\left(u_{i}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}+\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,-i}u_{-i}\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,-i}u_{-i}-\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{i,-i}u_{-i}\right)

\displaystyle=u_{i}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}+\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}\big[(I_{q}-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{i,-i}u_{-i}.

This implies the matrix expression.
∎

An important result from Lemma
C.2
is that it guarantees the following two identities. See the following corollary.

Corollary C.4
.

Suppose the conditions are the same as Lemma
C.3
, then we have

\Big[\mathrm{E}[X_{j}\mid X_{i}\cup\operatorname{Pa}_{B}(X_{i})]_{1}\mathds{1}(j\notin\operatorname{Pa}_{B}(X_{i}))\Big]_{j\neq i}=\big[(I-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i},

(C.1)

and

\Big[\mathrm{E}[X_{i}\mid X_{j}\cup\operatorname{Pa}_{B}(X_{j})]_{1}\mathds{1}(i\notin\operatorname{Pa}_{B}(X_{j}))\Big]_{j\neq i}=\big[(I-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{i,-i}.

(C.2)

Proof of Corollary
C.4
:

Compare the two expressions in Lemma
C.3
, take arbitrary
u\in\mathbb{R}^{q}
, we have

\big[(I-B^{\top})^{-1}\big]_{-i,i}=\Bigg[\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\mathrm{E}[X_{j}\mid do_{B}(X_{i}=x_{i})]\Bigg]_{j\neq i}.

On the other hand, the Pearl’s back-door adjustment implies

\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\mathrm{E}[X_{j}\mid do_{B}(X_{i}=x_{i})]=\mathrm{E}[X_{j}\mid X_{i}\cup\operatorname{Pa}_{B}(X_{i})]_{1}\mathds{1}(j\notin\operatorname{Pa}_{B}(X_{i})).

The two results give (
C.1
). Similarly, we can get (
C.2
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.2
:

Consider the M-estimator defined as

\big[\Theta^{*}\quad\theta^{*}\big]\in\arg\min_{[\Theta\ \theta]\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times t}}\operatorname{tr}\mathrm{E}\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]^{\top}

(C.3)

Since the distribution of
X
is non-degenerate,

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}\mathrm{E}\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\operatorname{tr}\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]^{\top}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]^{\top}\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[M-\Theta_{MC}C-\theta_{MA}A+(\Theta_{MC}-\Theta)C+(\theta_{MA}-\theta)A\big]^{\top}

\displaystyle\big[M-\Theta_{MC}C-\theta_{MA}A+(\Theta_{MC}-\Theta)C+(\theta_{MA}-\theta)A\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[e_{M}+(\Theta_{MC}-\Theta)C+(\theta_{MA}-\theta)A\big]^{\top}\big[e_{M}+(\Theta_{MC}-\Theta)C+(\theta_{MA}-\theta)A\big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}e_{M}^{\top}e_{M}+\underbrace{\mathrm{E}\big[(\Theta_{MC}-\Theta)C+(\theta_{MA}-\theta)A\big]^{\top}\big[(\Theta_{MC}-\Theta)C+(\theta_{MA}-\theta)A\big]}_{\geq 0}.

Therefore,
\big[\Theta_{MC}\quad\theta_{MA}\big]
is a solution of (
C.3
). It is enough to show that the solution
\big[\Theta^{*}\quad\theta^{*}\big]
is unique over MEC. Indeed, use the fact in Assumption
5.1
that the components of
\epsilon
are mutually independent,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]^{\top}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}e_{M}e_{M}^{\top}

\displaystyle=(I-B_{MM}^{\top})\operatorname{cov}(\epsilon)(I-B_{MM})

\displaystyle=\Big(\frac{1}{\sigma_{\ell_{1}}^{2}}[I-B_{MM}]_{\ell_{1}:}^{\top}[I-B_{MM}]_{:\ell_{2}}\Big)_{1\leq\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\leq p}.

When
\ell_{1}=\ell_{2}=\ell
,
\frac{1}{\sigma_{\ell_{1}}^{2}}[I-B_{MM}]_{\ell_{1}:}^{\top}[I-B_{MM}]_{:\ell_{2}}=\frac{1}{\sigma_{\ell}^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{p}\big(B_{MM}\big)_{k\ell}^{2}
. Thus, we know that

\operatorname{tr}\mathrm{E}\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]\big[M-\Theta C-\theta A\big]^{\top}=\sum_{1\leq j,k\leq p}\frac{1}{\sigma_{j}^{2}}(B_{MM})_{kj}^{2}.

Since any DAG in the same MEC shares the same skeleton, we know that the above optimization problem (
C.3
) is unique over MEC. The unique solution comes from the theory of M-estimator, see
Van de Geer et al. 2014
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
5.4
:

The basic idea is implying Lemma
C.1
. Denote the conditional version of the natural direct effect as

DE(c):=\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=1,M=m^{(0)}),C=c\}-\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=0),C=c\}.

Note that under Assumption
5.1
, we can write

A\leftarrow h(C,\epsilon_{A}),

M\leftarrow\left(I_{t-1}-B_{MM}^{\top}\right)^{-1}B_{MC}^{\top}C+\left(I_{t-1}-B_{MM}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\beta_{MA}A+\left(I_{t-1}-B_{MM}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\epsilon_{M}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=1,M=m^{(0)}),C=c\}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid A=1,M=m^{(0)},C=c\}

\displaystyle=\beta_{YC}^{\top}c+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM}^{\top}m^{(0)},

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=0),C=c\}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid A=0,C=c\}=\beta_{YC}^{\top}C+\beta_{YM}^{\top}m^{(0)}.

These equations together with the first display in Lemma
C.1
indicate

\displaystyle DE

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}DE(C)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}\Big[\left(\beta_{YC}^{\top}C+\alpha_{YA}+\beta_{YM}^{\top}m^{(0)}\right)-\left(\beta_{YC}^{\top}C+\beta_{YM}^{\top}m^{(0)}\right)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}\alpha_{YA}=\alpha_{YA}.

And similarly,

\displaystyle IE

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}IE(C)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}\Big[\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid A=0,M=m^{(1)},C\}-\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid A=0,C\}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}\Big[\beta_{YC}^{\top}C+\beta_{YM}^{\top}m^{(1)}-\left(\beta_{YC}^{\top}C+\beta_{YM}^{\top}m^{(0)}\right)\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}\Big[\beta_{YM}^{\top}\left(m^{(1)}-m^{(0)}\right)\Big]

\displaystyle\overset{\text{by }C\,\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 2.48856pt{}\kern 2.48856pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\textstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 2.48856pt{}\kern 2.48856pt\hbox{$\textstyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 1.59769pt{}\kern 1.59769pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 1.08406pt{}\kern 1.08406pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$}}}\,\epsilon_{M}}{=}\mathrm{E}_{C}\bigg[\left(I_{t-1}-B_{MM}^{\top}\right)^{-1}B_{MC}^{\top}C+\left(I_{t-1}-B_{MM}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\beta_{MA}\cdot 1

\displaystyle-\left(\left(I_{t-1}-B_{MM}^{\top}\right)^{-1}B_{MC}^{\top}C+\left(I_{t-1}-B_{MM}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\beta_{MA}\cdot 0\right)\bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}\left[\beta_{YM}^{\top}\left(I-B_{MM}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\beta_{MA}\right]=\beta_{YM}^{\top}\left(I-B_{MM}^{\top}\right)^{-1}\beta_{MA}.

This completes part (i).

For proving part (ii), we first note that Theorem F.4 in
Cai
et al. 2020
implies
DM_{j}
defined in Definition
3.2
is equivalent to Definition 3.2 in
Cai
et al. 2020
, which derives the expression
DM_{j}
directly from Part (i). On the other hand, recall we have
TM_{j}=TM_{j}(c)=IE-IE_{\mathcal{G}_{(-j)}}
. Hence,
TM_{j}
also has the exactly same as Definition 3.3 in
Cai
et al. 2020
by Corollary F.1 in
Cai
et al. 2020
. Therefore, we denote the total effect’s condition version as

TE(c)=\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=1),C=c\}-\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(A=0),C=c\}.

Then, by Theorem E.2 in
Watson
et al. 2023
, that is
TM_{j}(c)
can be interpreted as the effect of treatment
A
on the outcome
Y
that is mediated by the mediator
M_{j}
, or inversely as the change in total treatment effect caused by
M_{j}
being removed from the causal graph, i.e.

TM_{j}(c)=TE(c)-TE_{\mathcal{G}_{(-j)}}(c)=IE(c)-IE_{\mathcal{G}_{(-j)}}(c)=IE-IE_{\mathcal{G}_{(-j)}}.

Similar to Lemma
C.1
, one can show
TM_{j}=\mathrm{E}_{C}TM_{j}(C)
. Thus,

\displaystyle TM_{j}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}TM_{j}(C)

(C.4)

\displaystyle-\operatorname{cov}(\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(M_{j}=m_{j}+1),C\}-\mathrm{E}\{Y\mid do(M_{j}=m_{j}),C\},\Delta_{j}(C))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{C}TM_{j}(C)=IE-IE_{\mathcal{G}_{(-j)}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle IM_{j}

\displaystyle=TM_{j}-DM_{j}=IE-IE_{\mathcal{G}_{(-j)}}-DM_{j}

\displaystyle=\beta_{YM}^{\top}\big(I-B_{MM}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\beta_{MA}-\beta_{YM_{-j}}^{\top}\big(I-B_{M_{-j}M_{-j}}^{\top}\big)^{-1}\beta_{M_{-j}A}-DM_{j}

\displaystyle=\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}\theta_{MA,-j}-\beta^{\top}_{YM_{-}j}\theta_{M_{-j}A}=\beta_{YM,-j}^{\top}\big(\theta_{MA,-j}-\theta_{M_{-j}A}\big).

Finally, for proving (ii’), note that
Y
is still linear about
C
,
A
, and
M
, given
C=c
, the proof argument in proof of Theorem 3.1 and Theorem 3.2 of
Nandy
et al. 2017
still holds. Thus, fixed
C=c
, Proposition 2.1 in
Chakrabortty
et al. 2018
guarantees, that is

\displaystyle\eta_{j}(c)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\big[M_{j}\mid A\,\cup\,\{C=c\}\big]_{1}\times\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid M_{j},\operatorname{Pa}(M_{j}),A,\{C=c\}\big]_{1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{by \eqref{reg_exp}}}{=}\theta_{MA,j}\times\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid M_{j},\operatorname{Pa}(M_{j}),A,\{C=c\}\big]_{1}

for any fixed
C=c
, which combined with the expression of
DM_{j}
gives the result for the alternative expression for
IM_{j}
. Hence, we complete our proof.
∎

C.3
The Proofs of Section
6

Proof of Alternative Strategies:

Just by the definition, we can prove the equivalence for alternative strategy 1 as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{f(A=a^{\prime}\mid c)}Y\bigg]

\displaystyle=\int\frac{\mathds{1}(a=a^{\prime})}{f(a=a^{\prime}\mid c)}yf(c,a,y)\mathrm{d}(a,c)

\displaystyle=\int\frac{1}{f(a=a^{\prime}\mid c)}yf(y\mid a^{\prime},c)f(a=a^{\prime}\mid c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}(a,c)

\displaystyle=\int yf(y\mid a^{\prime},c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}(a,c)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}\kappa(a^{\prime},C),

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=0)}{f(A=0\mid C)}\bigg\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,M_{-j},C,M_{j}=m_{j}\big]f(m_{j}\mid A=a^{\prime},C=c)\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathds{1}(a=0)}{f(A=a\mid C)}f(c,a,m_{-j})\,\mathrm{d}c\,\mathrm{d}a\,\mathrm{d}m_{-j}

\displaystyle\times\bigg\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,M_{-j}=m_{-j},C=c,M_{j}=m_{j}\big]f(m_{j}\mid A=a^{\prime},C=c)\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{1}{f(A=0\mid C)}f(c)f(A=0\mid c)f(m_{-j}\mid C=c,A=0)\,\mathrm{d}c\,\mathrm{d}m_{-j}

\displaystyle\times\bigg\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,M_{-j}=m_{-j},C=c,M_{j}=m_{j}\big]f(m_{j}\mid A=a^{\prime},C=c)\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,M=m,C\big]f(m_{j}\mid C=c,A=a^{\prime})f(m_{-j}\mid C=c,A=0)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}m\,\mathrm{d}c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[\zeta_{j}(a^{\prime},0,C)\big]

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{f(A=a^{\prime}\mid c)}\left\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid C,A=a^{\prime},\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M}),M_{j}=m_{j}\big]f(m_{j}\mid C)\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}\right\}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{f(A=a^{\prime}\mid c)}f(c,a,\operatorname{pa}_{j})\,\mathrm{d}c\,\mathrm{d}a\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}

\displaystyle\times\left\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid C=c,A=a^{\prime},\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})=\operatorname{pa}_{j},M_{j}=m_{j}\big]f(m_{j}\mid C=c)\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid C=c,A=a^{\prime},\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})=\operatorname{pa}_{j},M_{j}=m_{j}\big]f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid A=a^{\prime},c)f(m_{j},c)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},m_{j},c\,;\,\mathcal{G}_{M})\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)=\mathrm{E}\big[\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C\,;\,\mathcal{G}_{M})\big].

Thus, all identities for alternative strategy 1 hold. Similarly, for Strategy 2 and Strategy 3, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{f(A=a^{\prime}\mid C)}\bigg\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,M_{j},C,M_{-j}=m_{-j}\big]f(m_{-j}\mid A=0,C)\,\mathrm{d}m_{-j}\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{f(A=a^{\prime}\mid C)}f(c,a,m_{j})\,\mathrm{d}c\,\mathrm{d}a\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}

\displaystyle\times\bigg\{\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,M_{j}=m_{j},C=c,M_{-j}=m_{-j}\big]f(m_{-j}\mid A=0,C=c)\,\mathrm{d}m_{-j}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{E}\big[Y\mid A=1,C=c,M=m\big]f(m_{-j}\mid A=0,c)f(m_{j}\mid A=a^{\prime},c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}m_{-j}\mathrm{d}c\,\mathrm{d}m_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[\zeta_{j}(a^{\prime},0,C)\big],

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=1)}{f(A=1\mid C)}\frac{f(M_{-j}\mid A=a^{\prime},C)}{f(M_{-j}\mid A=1,C,M_{j})}Y\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathds{1}(a=1)}{f(a=1\mid c)}\frac{f(m_{j}\mid a=1,c)f(m_{-j}\mid A=a^{\prime},c)}{f(m_{j}\mid a=1,c)f(m_{-j}\mid a=1,c,m_{j})}yf(c,a,m,y)\,\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{1}{f(a=1\mid c)}\frac{f(m_{j}\mid a=1,c)f(m_{-j}\mid A=a^{\prime},c)}{f(m\mid a=1,c)}

\displaystyle\times yf(y\mid a=1,c,m)f(m\mid a=1,c)f(a=1\mid c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int yf(y\mid a=1,c,m)f(m_{j}\mid a=1,c)f(m_{-j}\mid A=a^{\prime},c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{E}[Y\mid A=1,M=m,C=c]f(m_{j}\mid a=1,c)f(m_{-j}\mid A=a^{\prime},c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu=\mathrm{E}\big[\zeta_{j}(a^{\prime},0,C)\big],

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\bigg[\frac{\mathds{1}(A=a^{\prime})}{f(A=a^{\prime}\mid c)}f\big(\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\mid C,A=a^{\prime}\big)Y\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathds{1}(a=a^{\prime})}{f(a=a^{\prime}\mid c)}f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid c,a=a^{\prime})yf(c,a,m,y)\,\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\frac{1}{f(a=a^{\prime}\mid c)}f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid c,a=a^{\prime})yf(y\mid a=a^{\prime},c,m_{j},\operatorname{pa}_{j})f(m_{j}\mid a=a^{\prime},c)f(a=a^{\prime}\mid c)f(c)\,\mathrm{d}\mu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int yf(y\mid c,a=a^{\prime},m_{j},\operatorname{pa}_{j})f(\operatorname{pa}_{j}\mid c,a=a^{\prime})f(m_{j},c)\,\mathrm{d}\operatorname{pa}_{j}\,\mathrm{d}(m_{j},c)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\big[\varrho_{j}(a^{\prime},M_{j},C\,;\,\mathcal{G}_{M})\big],

which complete the calculations.
∎

C.4
Proof of Section
7.1

Denote
a=\operatorname{plim}a_{n}
if
a_{n}\,\xlongrightarrow{\mathrm{P}}\,a
.

Lemma C.5
.

Let
X
are mean-zero random vector and
Y
are univariate random variable. Suppose
\mathrm{E}(\epsilon\mid X)=0
and
\operatorname{var}(\epsilon\mid X)=\sigma^{2}
is free of
X
in the regression

Y=\beta^{\top}X+\epsilon=\beta_{1}^{\top}X_{1}+\beta_{2}^{\top}X_{2}+\epsilon.

Suppose
X\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, and
\widehat{\beta}
is the OLS estimator with sample size
p<n
,

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}\big)=\left[\operatorname{plim}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}^{\top}\right]^{1/2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{i}+o_{p}(1)\,\rightsquigarrow\,\mathcal{N}\left(0,\operatorname{plim}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{i}^{2}\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}^{\top}\right]\right),

where
\{\widehat{\epsilon}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are the residuals and
\{\varepsilon_{i}\}_{i=1}^{n}
are the i.i.d.
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2}I)
error vector independent with
X
.

We first note, we have

\widehat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}=\big[\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}(I_{n}-P_{\mathbf{X}_{2}})\mathbf{X}_{1}\big]^{-1}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}(I_{n}-P_{\mathbf{X}_{2}})\bm{\varepsilon}=\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}\bm{\varepsilon}.

(C.5)

\left[n\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}^{\top}\right]^{-1}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}-\left[\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{2}\right]\left[\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{2}\right]^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right],

we have

\displaystyle\left[n\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}^{\top}\right]^{-1}-\left[\widehat{\operatorname{var}}(X_{1})-\widehat{\operatorname{cov}}(X_{1},X_{2})\widehat{\operatorname{var}}^{-1}(X_{2})\widehat{\operatorname{cov}}(X_{2},X_{1})\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}-\widehat{\operatorname{var}}(X_{1})\right)

\displaystyle-\left(\left[\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{2}\right]\left[\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{2}\right]^{-1}\left[\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{X}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{1}\right]-\widehat{\operatorname{cov}}(X_{1},X_{2})\widehat{\operatorname{var}}^{-1}(X_{2})\widehat{\operatorname{cov}}(X_{2},X_{1})\right)

\displaystyle=0,

which gives

n\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}^{\top}=\left[\widehat{\operatorname{var}}(X_{1})-\widehat{\operatorname{cov}}(X_{1},X_{2})\widehat{\operatorname{var}}^{-1}(X_{2})\widehat{\operatorname{cov}}(X_{2},X_{1})\right]^{-1}.

On the other hand, from (
C.5
), we get that

\displaystyle\widehat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}\bm{\varepsilon}.

Since
\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\beta}-\beta\big)
is asymptotic normal and
\mathrm{E}(\epsilon^{2}\mid X)=\sigma^{2}
, its asymptotic variance is

\displaystyle\left(\mathrm{E}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}X_{1}X_{1}^{\top}&X_{1}X_{2}^{\top}\\
X_{2}X_{1}^{\top}&X_{2}X_{2}^{\top}\end{array}\right]\right)^{-1}\mathrm{E}\left(\epsilon_{Y}^{2}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}X_{1}X_{1}^{\top}&X_{1}X_{2}^{\top}\\
X_{2}X_{1}^{\top}&X_{2}X_{2}^{\top}\end{array}\right]\right)\left(\mathrm{E}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}X_{1}X_{1}^{\top}&X_{1}X_{2}^{\top}\\
X_{2}X_{1}^{\top}&X_{2}X_{2}^{\top}\end{array}\right]\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\operatorname{var}(X_{1})&\operatorname{cov}(X_{1},X_{2})\\
\operatorname{cov}(X_{2},X_{1})&\operatorname{var}(X_{2})\end{array}\right]\right)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sigma^{2}\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\left({\operatorname{var}}(X_{1})-{\operatorname{cov}}(X_{1},X_{2}){\operatorname{var}}^{-1}(X_{2}){\operatorname{cov}}(X_{2},X_{1})\right)^{-1}&*\\
&*\end{array}\right].

Combining the above result, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}\big)

\displaystyle\rightsquigarrow\,\mathcal{N}\left(0,\sigma^{2}\left[{\operatorname{var}}(X_{1})-{\operatorname{cov}}(X_{1},X_{2}){\operatorname{var}}^{-1}(X_{2}){\operatorname{cov}}(X_{2},X_{1})\right]^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}\left(0,\sigma^{2}\operatorname{plim}n\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{N}\left(0,\operatorname{plim}\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{i}^{2}}{n}\operatorname{plim}n\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}\widehat{\Gamma}_{X_{1},X_{2}}^{\top}\right),

by the fact that
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{i}^{2}\,\xlongrightarrow{\mathrm{P}}\,\mathrm{E}\epsilon^{2}=\sigma^{2}
. The asymptotic linear expression comes from the above display directly.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.3
:

From the regression (
5.2
), we know that

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\beta}_{YC}\\
\widehat{\alpha}_{YA}\\
\widehat{\beta}_{YM}\end{array}\right)-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\beta}_{YC}\\
{\alpha}_{YA}\\
{\beta}_{YM}\end{array}\right)=\left(\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{C}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{A}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{M}^{\top}\end{array}\right]\left[\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbf{C}&\mathbf{A}&\mathbf{M}\end{array}\right]\right)^{-1}\left[\begin{array}[]{c}\mathbf{C}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{A}^{\top}\\
\mathbf{M}^{\top}\end{array}\right]\bm{\epsilon}_{Y}.

Thus, by (
C.5
), we have

\widehat{\alpha}_{YA}-\alpha_{YA}=\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}\bm{\epsilon}_{Y}

and

\widehat{\beta}_{YM}-\beta_{YM}=\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}\bm{\epsilon}_{Y}.

where
\operatorname{var}(\epsilon_{Y}\mid C,A,M)=\sigma_{Y}^{2}
is a constant free of
C,A,M
. Similarly,

\widehat{\theta}_{MA}^{\top}-\theta_{MA}^{\top}=\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,C}\mathbf{e}_{M}

it is worthy to note that

\displaystyle\operatorname{var}(e_{M}\mid C,A)

\displaystyle=\operatorname{var}\big[(I-B_{MM}^{\top})\epsilon_{M}\mid(I-B_{CC}^{\top})\epsilon_{C},\beta_{AC}^{\top}(I-B_{CC}^{\top})\epsilon_{C}+\epsilon_{A}\big]

\displaystyle=\operatorname{var}((I-B_{MM}^{\top})\epsilon_{M})=\sigma_{\epsilon_{M}}^{2}(I-B_{MM}^{\top})(I-B_{MM})

is also a constant free of
C
and
A
by
\epsilon_{M}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 3.46875pt{}\kern 3.46875pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\textstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 3.46875pt{}\kern 3.46875pt\hbox{$\textstyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 2.48856pt{}\kern 2.48856pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 1.78203pt{}\kern 1.78203pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$}}}(C,\epsilon_{A})
.
Therefore, by Lemma
C.5
, we have

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\alpha}_{YA}-\alpha_{YA}\big)\,\rightsquigarrow\,\mathcal{N}\left(0,\operatorname{plim}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{Y,i}^{2}\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}^{\top}\right]\right),

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\beta}_{YM}-\beta_{YM}\big)\,\rightsquigarrow\,\mathcal{N}\left(0,\operatorname{plim}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{Y,i}^{2}\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}^{\top}\right]\right),

and

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\alpha}_{YA}-\alpha_{YA}\\
\widehat{\beta}_{YM}-\beta_{YM}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}\\
\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}\end{array}\right)\bm{\epsilon}_{Y}

\displaystyle\rightsquigarrow\,N\left(0,\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}*&\operatorname{plim}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{Y,i}^{2}\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}^{\top}\right]\\
\operatorname{plim}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{Y,i}^{2}\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}^{\top}\right]&*\end{array}\right]\right).

Besides,

\sqrt{n}\big(\widehat{\theta}_{MA}-\theta_{MA}\big)\,\rightsquigarrow\,\mathcal{N}\left(0,\operatorname{plim}\widehat{\mathbf{e}}_{M}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{e}}_{M}\Gamma_{A,C}\Gamma_{A,C}^{\top}\right).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{DE}^{OLS}-DE\\
\widehat{IE}^{OLS}-IE\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
\theta_{MA}^{\top}\quad\beta_{YM}^{\top}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\alpha}_{YA}-\alpha_{YA}\\
\widehat{\beta}_{YM}-\beta_{YM}\\
\widehat{\theta}_{MA}-\widehat{\theta}_{MA}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle\rightsquigarrow\,N\left(0,\left[\begin{array}[]{cc}\operatorname{plim}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{Y,i}^{2}\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}^{\top}\right]&\operatorname{plim}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{Y,i}^{2}\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}^{\top}\right]\theta_{MA}\\
\operatorname{plim}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{Y,i}^{2}\theta_{MA}^{\top}\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}\right]&{\theta}_{MA}^{\top}\bigg[\operatorname{plim}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{Y,i}^{2}\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}^{\top}\right]\bigg]\theta_{MA}\\
&+\beta_{YM}^{\top}\bigg[\operatorname{plim}\widehat{\mathbf{e}}_{M}^{\top}\widehat{\mathbf{e}}_{M}\Gamma_{A,C}\Gamma_{A,C}^{\top}\bigg]\beta_{YM}\end{array}\right]\right)

by
\epsilon_{Y}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\displaystyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 3.46875pt{}\kern 3.46875pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\textstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 3.46875pt{}\kern 3.46875pt\hbox{$\textstyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 2.48856pt{}\kern 2.48856pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle\perp$}}{\hbox to0.0pt{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$}\hss}\kern 1.78203pt{}\kern 1.78203pt\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$}}}e_{M}
, which gives the result for
\widehat{DE}^{OLS}
as well as the result for
\widehat{IE}^{OLS}
when
\theta_{MA}
and
\beta_{YM}
are not both equal to zero. When
\theta_{MA}=\beta_{YM}=0
, we write

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{IE}^{\text{OLS}}-IE)}{\sqrt{\widehat{\beta}_{YM}^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta_{MA}}\widehat{\beta}_{YM}+\widehat{\theta}_{MA}^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\beta_{YM}}\widehat{\theta}_{MA}}}=\frac{\sqrt{n}\widehat{\theta}_{MA}^{\top}\sqrt{n}\beta_{YM}}{\sqrt{\sqrt{n}\widehat{\beta}_{YM}^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta_{MA}}\sqrt{n}\widehat{\beta}_{YM}+\sqrt{n}\widehat{\theta}_{MA}^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\beta_{YM}}\sqrt{n}\widehat{\theta}_{MA}}}

which leads to the result by the continuous mapping theorem. Similar method applies to the proof of
\widehat{DM}_{j}^{OLS}
. Therefore, we have
Then

\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{DE}^{\text{OLS}}-DE)}{\sqrt{\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,(M,C)}^{\top}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\widehat{\epsilon}_{Y,i}^{2}}}\,\rightsquigarrow\,\mathcal{N}(0,1).

and

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{n}(\widehat{IE}^{\text{OLS}}-IE)}{\sqrt{\widehat{\beta}_{YM}^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta_{MA}}\widehat{\beta}_{YM}+\widehat{\theta}_{MA}^{\top}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\beta_{YM}}\widehat{\theta}_{MA}}}\,\rightsquigarrow\,\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\displaystyle\frac{Z_{\theta}^{\top}Z_{\beta}}{\sqrt{Z_{\theta}^{\top}\Sigma_{\beta}Z_{\theta}+Z_{\beta}^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}Z_{\beta}}}\qquad,&\text{if }\theta_{MA}=\beta_{YM}=0,\\
\mathcal{N}(0,1),&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

where

\left(\begin{array}[]{c}Z_{\theta}\\
Z_{\beta}\end{array}\right)\,\sim\,\mathcal{N}\left(0,\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\Sigma_{\theta_{MA}}&\\
&\Sigma_{\beta_{YM}}\end{array}\right)\right),\qquad\Sigma_{\theta_{MA}}=\operatorname{plim}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\theta_{MA}},\qquad\Sigma_{\beta_{YM}}=\operatorname{plim}\widehat{\Sigma}_{\beta_{YM}}.

Note that the
\frac{Z_{\theta}^{\top}Z_{\beta}}{\sqrt{Z_{\theta}^{\top}\Sigma_{\beta}Z_{\theta}+Z_{\beta}^{\top}\Sigma_{\theta}Z_{\beta}}}
is much more concentrated around zero compared to the standard normal distribution
Chakrabortty
et al. 2018
, we get the "
\geq
" instead of "
=
" for the confidence interval of
\widehat{IE}^{OLS}
. Similar result can be applied to
\widehat{DM}_{j}^{OLS}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
7.8
:

Let
\mu=\mathrm{E}X
is the mean of
X
. Denote

\xi_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M}):=\mathrm{E}^{\text{reg}}\big[Y\mid M_{j}\cup\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\cup A\cup C\big]_{1}

and corresponding quantities

\overline{\xi}_{j}(\mathcal{C}_{M}):=\frac{1}{\#\operatorname{MEC}(\mathcal{C}_{M})}\sum_{\mathcal{G}_{M}\in\operatorname{MEC}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})}\mathrm{E}^{\text{reg}}\big[Y\mid M_{j}\cup\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\cup A\cup C\big]_{1}

and

\widetilde{\xi}_{j}(\mathcal{C}_{M}):=\frac{1}{\#\operatorname{MEC}(\mathcal{C}_{M})}\sum_{\mathcal{G}_{M}\in\operatorname{MEC}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})}\widehat{\xi}(\mathcal{G}_{M}),\qquad\widehat{\xi}(\mathcal{G}_{M}):=\widehat{\mathrm{E}}^{\text{reg}}\big[Y\mid M_{j}\cup\operatorname{Pa}_{j}(\mathcal{G}_{M})\cup A\cup C\big]_{1}

From (ii’) in Proposition
5.4
, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{{IM}}_{j}^{OLS}-\overline{IM}_{j}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{MA,j}\Big(\widetilde{\xi}_{j}(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{M})-\widehat{\beta}_{YM,j}\Big)-{\theta}_{MA,j}\Big(\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-{\beta}_{YM,j}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{MA,j}\Big(\big(\widetilde{\xi}_{j}(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{M})-\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})\big)-\big(\widehat{\beta}_{YM,j}-{\beta}_{YM,j}\big)\Big)+\big(\widehat{\theta}_{MA,j}-{\theta}_{MA,j}\big)\Big(\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-{\beta}_{YM,j}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widehat{\theta}_{MA,j}\Big(\widetilde{\xi}_{j}(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{M})-\widetilde{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})\Big)+\widehat{\theta}_{MA,j}\Big(\widetilde{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})\Big)

\displaystyle-\widehat{\theta}_{MA,j}\big(\widehat{\beta}_{YM,j}-{\beta}_{YM,j}\big)+\big(\widehat{\theta}_{MA,j}-{\theta}_{MA,j}\big)\Big(\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-{\beta}_{YM,j}\Big)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle D_{j,1}+D_{j,2}-D_{j,3}+D_{j,4}

for
j\in[p]
. What we want to do next is finding the asymptotic expression of
\widehat{{IM}}_{j}-\overline{IM}_{j}
. First, for any non-negative sequence
\{a_{n}\}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{P}\left(a_{n}\max_{j\in[p]}\big|D_{j,1}\big|>\varepsilon\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{P}\Big(\exists\,j\in[p]:\widetilde{\xi}_{j}(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{M})-\widetilde{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})\neq 0\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\mathrm{P}\Big(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{M}\neq\mathcal{C}_{M}\Big)\overset{\text{by Assumption \eqref{ass_nor_cpdag}}}{\longrightarrow}0.

Thus, we have
\max_{j\in[p]}D_{j,1}=o_{p}(a_{n}^{-1})
. We first consider the case at least one
\theta_{MA,j}
or
\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-{\beta}_{YM,j}
is not equal to zero. From the proof of Theorem
7.3
and Lemma
C.5
, we know that

D_{j,2}={\theta}_{MA,j}\Big(\widetilde{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})\Big)+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

\displaystyle D_{j,3}

\displaystyle=\theta_{MA,j}\Big[\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}\bm{\epsilon}_{Y}\Big]_{j}+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\theta_{MA,j}\left[\operatorname{plim}\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}\widehat{\Gamma}_{M,(C,A)}^{\top}\right]_{jj}^{1/2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\epsilon_{Y}+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right),

and

\displaystyle D_{j,4}

\displaystyle=\big(\widehat{\theta}_{MA,j}^{\top}-{\theta}_{MA,j}^{\top}\big)\Big(\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-{\beta}_{YM,j}\Big)

\displaystyle=\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,C}\mathbf{e}_{M}\right]_{:j}\Big(\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-{\beta}_{YM,j}\Big)+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\Big(\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-{\beta}_{YM,j}\Big)\left[\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,C}\bm{\epsilon}_{M}(I_{p}-B_{MM})^{-1}\right]_{:j}+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\Big(\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-{\beta}_{YM,j}\Big)\left[\operatorname{plim}\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,C}\widehat{\Gamma}_{A,C}^{\top}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\Big[(I_{p}-B_{MM}^{\top})^{-1}\Sigma_{M}(I_{p}-B_{MM})^{-1}\Big]_{jj}^{1/2}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\varepsilon_{M,j,i}+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{n}\right).

uniformly in
j\in[p]
, where
\varepsilon_{M}:=\Sigma_{M}^{-1/2}\epsilon_{M}
. We denote
X_{S_{M,j,1}},\ldots,X_{S_{M,j,L_{\text{distinct},j}}}
be distinct parent sets of
M_{j}
with
m_{j,1},\ldots,m_{j,L_{\text{distinct},j}}
times. Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{j}

\displaystyle:=\big\{S_{jr}=(j+t,1,\ldots,t,X_{S_{M,j,r}}):r\in\{1,\ldots,L_{\text{distinct},j}\},\,t\notin X_{S_{M,j,r}}\big\}

\displaystyle\cup\,\big\{S_{jr}=(j+t,1,\ldots,t-1,X_{S_{M,j,r}}):r\in\{1,\ldots,L_{\text{distinct},j}\},\,t\in X_{S_{M,j,r}}\big\}\bm{\epsilon}_{Y}

with
a_{S_{jr}}=(m_{jr}/L_{j},0,\ldots,0)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}
and
L_{j}=\sum_{r=1}^{L_{\text{distinct},j}}m_{jr}
. Since

\sum_{S_{jr}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}}\|a_{S_{jr}}\|_{2}=\sum_{r=1}^{L_{\text{distinct},j}}\frac{m_{jr}}{L_{j}}=1

for any
j\in[p]
, we can apply REMARK 5.2 in
Chakrabortty
et al. 2018
, we have

\widetilde{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})-\overline{\xi}_{j}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}^{(j)}({\mathcal{C}}_{M})+o_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\right)

where
